**Ye**[^cite_note-name-1] ([/jeɪ/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English) [*YAY*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Pronunciation_respelling_key); born **Kanye Omari West** [/ˈkɑːnjeɪ oʊˈmɑːri/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English) [*KAHN-yay oh-MAH-ree*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Pronunciation_respelling_key), June 8, 1977) is an American rapper, songwriter, and record producer. He has been listed among the greatest rappers of all time[^cite_note-7] and referred to as one of the most prominent figures in [hip-hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hip-hop).[^cite_note-8] His music, characterized by frequent stylistic shifts, has been credited with facilitating the emergence of rappers who did not conform to [gangsta rap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gangsta_rap) conventions. He is also known for his controversial public persona, including [his polarizing cultural and political commentary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Views_of_Kanye_West).[^cite_note-9][^cite_note-10]

West was born in [Atlanta](3138.md) and raised in [Chicago](6886.md). After dropping out of college to pursue a music career, he became a producer for [Jay-Z](162870.md)'s [Roc-A-Fella Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roc-A-Fella_Records) and garnered recognition for his "[chipmunk soul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipmunk_soul)" production style before signing with the label as a recording artist. His first two studio albums, *[The College Dropout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_College_Dropout)* (2004) and *[Late Registration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Registration)* (2005), received critical acclaim and became his first of eleven US [*Billboard* 200](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard_200) number-one albums. *[Rolling Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone)* ranked *[Graduation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graduation_\(Kanye_West_album\))* (2007), *[808s & Heartbreaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/808s_&_Heartbreaks)* (2008), *[My Beautiful Dark Twisted Fantasy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/My_Beautiful_Dark_Twisted_Fantasy)* (2010), and *[Yeezus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeezus)* (2013) among the [greatest albums of all time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone's_500_Greatest_Albums_of_All_Time). He has five US [*Billboard* Hot 100](423161.md) number-one singles―"[Slow Jamz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slow_Jamz)" (2003), "[Gold Digger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gold_Digger_\(Kanye_West_song\))" (2005), "[Stronger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stronger_\(Kanye_West_song\))" (2007), "[E.T.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E.T._\(song\))" (2011, as a featured artist), and "[Carnival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnival_\(%C2%A5\$_song\))" (2024)―and was the [first rapper to top the chart across three decades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Billboard_Hot_100_chart_achievements_and_milestones#Most_consecutive_decades_charting_a_number-one_song).

West's life has been the subject of significant media coverage. He has been a frequent source of controversy due to his conduct on [social media](5897742.md), at award shows, and in public settings, as well as his comments on the [music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_industry) and [fashion industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fashion_industry), [U.S. politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._politics), [race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Race_and_ethnicity_in_the_United_States), and [slavery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery_in_the_United_States). His [Christian](5211.md) faith, relationships, feuds with other celebrities, and mental health have also been topics of media attention.[^cite_note-11][^cite_note-Graham2019-12][^cite_note-Schaffstall-13] From 2014 to 2022, he was married to [Kim Kardashian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Kardashian), with whom he has four children. In 2020, West ran [an unsuccessful independent presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanye_West_2020_presidential_campaign). From 2022 to 2025, West drew widespread condemnation—and lost sponsors and partnerships—for expressing [antisemitic](1078.md) views and support for [Nazism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazism); he praised [Adolf Hitler](2731583.md), used [swastika](45943.md) imagery, and [denied the Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_denial).

West is one of the [best-selling music artists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_best-selling_music_artists), with 160 million records sold.[^cite_note-14] He has 24 [Grammy Awards](44635.md), making him one of the [most awarded artists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammy_Award_records#Most_Grammys_won) at the award show. *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))* named him one of the [100 most influential people in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100) in 2005 and 2015.[^cite_note-15][^cite_note-16] *Rolling Stone* also named him one of the "[100 Greatest Songwriters of All Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone's_100_Greatest_Songwriters_of_All_Time)".[^cite_note-17] Beyond his musical career, he has collaborated with [Nike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike,_Inc.), [Louis Vuitton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Vuitton), and [Gap Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gap_Inc.) on clothing and footwear, and led the [Yeezy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas_Yeezy) collaboration with [Adidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas).

## Early life

Kanye Omari West was born on June 8, 1977, in Atlanta, Georgia.[^cite_note-20] After his parents divorced when he was three years old, he moved with his mother to [Chicago](6886.md), Illinois.[^cite_note-21][^cite_note-leader-22] His father, Ray West, is a former [Black Panther](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Panther_Party) and was one of the first black photojournalists at *[The Atlanta Journal-Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Atlanta_Journal-Constitution)*. Ray later became a Christian counselor,[^cite_note-leader-22] and in 2006, opened the Good Water Store and Café in [Lexington Park, Maryland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lexington_Park,_Maryland), with [startup capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Startup_capital) from his son.[^cite_note-23][^cite_note-wapo-water-24] West's mother, [Donda C. West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donda_C._West) (née Williams),[^cite_note-25] was an English professor at [Clark Atlanta University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clark_Atlanta_University) and the Chair of the English Department at [Chicago State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_State_University) before retiring to serve as his manager.[^cite_note-26]

West was raised in a middle-class environment, attending Polaris School for Individual Education[^cite_note-KWstBio5-27] in suburban [Oak Lawn, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oak_Lawn,_Illinois), after living in Chicago.[^cite_note-cant_ignore-28] At the age of 10, West moved with his mother to [Nanjing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanjing), China, where she was teaching at [Nanjing University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanjing_University) as a [Fulbright Scholar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fulbright_Program).[^cite_note-29] According to his mother, West was the only foreigner in his class, but he settled in well and quickly picked up the language, although he has since forgotten most of it.[^cite_note-30] When asked about his grades in high school, West replied, "I got A's and B's."[^cite_note-31]

West demonstrated an affinity for the arts at an early age; he began writing poetry when he was five years old.[^cite_note-Chicago-32] West started rapping in the third grade and began making musical compositions in the seventh grade, eventually selling them to other artists.[^cite_note-KanplicatedPg2-33] West crossed paths with producer [No I.D.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No_I.D.), who became West's friend and mentor.[^cite_note-\:0-34]<sup>: 557 </sup> After graduating from high school, West received a scholarship to attend Chicago's [American Academy of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Academy_of_Art) in 1997 and began taking painting classes. Shortly after, he transferred to Chicago State University to study English. At age 20, he dropped out to pursue his musical career.[^cite_note-35] This disappointed his mother, who was also a professor at the university, although she would later accept the decision.[^cite_note-\:0-34]<sup>: 558 </sup>

## Musical career

### 1996–2002: Early work and Roc-A-Fella

West began his early production career in the mid-1990s, creating beats primarily for burgeoning local artists in the Chicago area. He received his first official production credits at age nineteen, when he produced eight tracks on *Down to Earth*, the 1996 debut album of Chicago-based [underground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Underground_hip-hop) rapper Grav.[^cite_note-36] In 1998, West was the first producer signed to the management-production company [Hip Hop Since 1978](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hip_Hop_Since_1978), founded by Gee Roberson and Kyambo "Hip-Hop" Joshua.[^cite_note-37] For a time, West acted as a ghost producer for [Deric "D-Dot" Angelettie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deric_%22D-Dot%22_Angelettie). Due to his association with Angelettie, West was not able to release a solo album. This led to him forming the Go-Getters, a hip-hop group composed of him and fellow Chicago natives [GLC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GLC_\(rapper\)), Timmy G, and Arrowstar.[^cite_note-Barber-38][^cite_note-39] The Go-Getters independently released their first and only studio album, *World Record Holders*, in 1999, through West's company, [Konman Productions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konman_Productions).[^cite_note-Barber-38] West spent much of the late 1990s further producing for several musical acts.[^cite_note-Saddleback-40] He produced "My Life" on [Foxy Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foxy_Brown_\(rapper\))'s second studio album *[Chyna Doll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chyna_Doll_\(album\))* (1999), which became the second hip-hop album by a female rapper to peak atop the US *Billboard* 200 chart.[^cite_note-Saddleback-40]



West received early acclaim for his production work on [Jay-Z](162870.md)'s *[The Blueprint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Blueprint)*. The two are pictured here in 2011.

In 2000, West began producing for artists on [Roc-A-Fella Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roc-A-Fella_Records) as an in-house producer. West is often credited with revitalizing Jay-Z's career with extensive contributions to his 2001 album *[The Blueprint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Blueprint)*,[^cite_note-Mitchum-41] which *Rolling Stone* ranked among their list of greatest hip-hop albums.[^cite_note-RS500-42] West produced songs for label cohorts such as [Beanie Sigel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beanie_Sigel) and [Freeway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freeway_\(rapper\)), but also produced beats which were used by artists on other labels including [Ludacris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ludacris), [Alicia Keys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alicia_Keys), and [Janet Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janet_Jackson).[^cite_note-Mitchum-41][^cite_note-auto-43] Meanwhile, West struggled to attain a record deal as a rapper.[^cite_note-Road-44] Multiple record companies, including [Capitol Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitol_Records),[^cite_note-KanplicatedPg2-33] denied or ignored him because he did not portray the [gangsta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gangsta_rap) image prominent in mainstream hip-hop at the time.[^cite_note-\:0-34]<sup>: 556 </sup> Desperate to keep West from defecting to another label, then-label head [Damon Dash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Damon_Dash) reluctantly signed West to Roc-A-Fella as a recording artist.[^cite_note-\:0-34]<sup>: 556 </sup>[^cite_note-45]

After a 2002 car accident shattered his jaw,[^cite_note-wsws-46][^cite_note-47] West was inspired; two weeks after being admitted to the hospital, he recorded "[Through the Wire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Through_the_Wire)" at the [Record Plant Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Record_Plant_Studios) with his jaw still wired shut.[^cite_note-wsws-46] The song was first included on West's debut [mixtape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mixtape) *[Get Well Soon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Get_Well_Soon_\(mixtape\))*, which was released in December 2002.[^cite_note-48] At the same time, West announced that he was working on an album titled *[The College Dropout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_College_Dropout)*, whose overall theme was to "make your own decisions. Don't let society tell you, 'This is what you have to do.'"[^cite_note-49]

### 2003–2006: *The College Dropout* and *Late Registration*

Main articles: [*The College Dropout*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_College_Dropout) and [*Late Registration*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Registration)



West in [Portland](23503.md) in December 2005 as a supporting act for [U2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U2) on their [Vertigo Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vertigo_Tour)

West recorded the remainder of *The College Dropout* in Los Angeles while recovering from the car accident. It was leaked months before its release date,[^cite_note-Road-44] and West used the opportunity to remix, remaster, and revise the album before its release;[^cite_note-50] West added new verses, [string arrangements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/String_arrangements), [gospel choirs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gospel_choir), and improved drum programming.[^cite_note-Road-44] The album was postponed three times from its initial date in August 2003,[^cite_note-51][^cite_note-52] and was eventually released in February 2004, reaching No. 2 on the *Billboard* 200 as his debut single, "[Through the Wire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Through_the_Wire)" peaked at No. 15 while on the *Billboard* Hot 100 chart for five weeks.[^cite_note-acharts-53] "[Slow Jamz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slow_Jamz)", his second single, featuring [Twista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twista) and [Jamie Foxx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamie_Foxx), became the three musicians' first No. 1 hit. *The College Dropout* received critical acclaim, was nominated for the top album of the year by *[American Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Music_Awards_of_2004)* and *[Billboard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Billboard_Music_Awards)*,[^cite_note-54][^cite_note-55] and has consistently been ranked among the great hip-hop works and debut albums by artists.[^cite_note-USAToday-56][^cite_note-NYTimes-57]

"[Jesus Walks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesus_Walks)", the album's fourth single, reached the top 20 of the *Billboard* pop charts, despite industry executives' predictions that a song containing such blatant declarations of faith would never make it to the radio.[^cite_note-USAToday-56][^cite_note-NYTimes-57] *The College Dropout* was certified [triple platinum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RIAA_certification) in the U.S., and garnered West 10 Grammy nominations, including [Album of the Year](196513.md), and [Best Rap Album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Rap_Album) (which it received).[^cite_note-grammy-58] During this period, West founded [GOOD Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GOOD_Music), a record label and management company that housed affiliate artists and producers, such as No I.D. and [John Legend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Legend),[^cite_note-Sheffield-59] and produced singles for [Brandy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandy_\(musician\)), [Common](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_\(rapper\)), Legend, and [Slum Village](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slum_Village).[^cite_note-auto-43]

West invested \$2 million and took over a year to make his second album.[^cite_note-60] West was inspired by *[Roseland NYC Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roseland_NYC_Live)*, a 1998 live album by English [trip hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trip_hop) group [Portishead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portishead_\(band\)), produced with the [New York Philharmonic Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Philharmonic_Orchestra),[^cite_note-Praise-61] incorporating [string arrangements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/String_arrangement) into his hip-hop production. Though West had not been able to afford many live instruments around the time of his debut album, the money from his commercial success enabled him to hire a [string orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/String_orchestra) for his second album *[Late Registration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Registration)*.[^cite_note-Praise-61] West collaborated with American [film score](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_score) composer [Jon Brion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Brion), who served as the album's co-executive producer for several tracks.[^cite_note-Pilot-62][^cite_note-Pg124-63] *Late Registration* sold over 2.3 million units in the United States alone by the end of 2005 and was considered by industry observers as the only successful major album release of the fall season, which had been plagued by steadily declining CD sales.[^cite_note-64]

When his song "[Touch the Sky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Touch_the_Sky_\(Kanye_West_song\))" failed to win Best Video at the 2006 [MTV Europe Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Europe_Music_Awards), West went onto the stage as the award was being presented to [Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justice_\(band\)) and [Simian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simian_\(band\)) for "[We Are Your Friends](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/We_Are_Your_Friends_\(song\))" and argued that he should have won the award instead.[^cite_note-65][^cite_note-66] Hundreds of news outlets worldwide criticized the outburst. On November 7, 2006, West apologized for this outburst publicly during his performance as support act for [U2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U2) for their Vertigo concert in [Brisbane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brisbane).[^cite_note-67] He later spoofed the incident on the 33rd-season premiere of *Saturday Night Live* in September 2007.[^cite_note-snlarchives-68]

### 2007–2009: *Graduation*, *808s & Heartbreak*, and VMAs incident

Main articles: [Graduation (album)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graduation_\(album\)), [808s & Heartbreak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/808s_&_Heartbreak), [Kanye West MTV Video Music Awards controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanye_West_MTV_Video_Music_Awards_controversy), and [Kanye West–Taylor Swift feud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanye_West%E2%80%93Taylor_Swift_feud)



West (center) working on [808s & Heartbreak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/808s_&_Heartbreak) with producer and former mentor [No I.D.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No_I.D.) (left) in October 2008

West's third studio album, *[Graduation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graduation_\(album\))*, was released in September 2007. The album debuted at number one on the U.S. *Billboard* 200 chart and selling 957,000 copies in its first week.[^cite_note-firstday-69] *Graduation* continued the string of critical and commercial successes by West, and the album's lead single, "[Stronger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stronger_\(Kanye_West_song\))", garnered his third number-one hit.[^cite_note-70] "Stronger", which samples French house duo [Daft Punk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daft_Punk), has been accredited to not only encouraging other hip-hop artists to incorporate [house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_\(music\)) and [electronica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronica) elements into their music, but also for playing a part in the revival of [disco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disco) and [electro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electro_music)-infused music in the late 2000s.[^cite_note-71] His mother's death in November 2007[^cite_note-72] and the end of his engagement to Alexis Phifer[^cite_note-73] profoundly affected West, who set off for his 2008 [Glow in the Dark Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glow_in_the_Dark_Tour) shortly thereafter.[^cite_note-74]

Recorded mostly in [Honolulu](13887.md), Hawaii in three weeks,[^cite_note-TheFader-75] West announced his fourth album, *[808s & Heartbreak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/808s_&_Heartbreak)*, at the [2008 MTV Video Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_MTV_Video_Music_Awards), where he performed its lead single, "[Love Lockdown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_Lockdown)". Music audiences were taken aback by the uncharacteristic production style and the presence of [AutoTune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AutoTune), which typified the pre-release response to the record.[^cite_note-Observer-76] *808s & Heartbreak* was released by [Island Def Jam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Island_Def_Jam) in November 2008.[^cite_note-Question-77][^cite_note-Sales-78] Upon its release, the lead single "Love Lockdown" debuted at number three on the *Billboard* Hot 100,[^cite_note-79] while follow-up single "[Heartless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heartless_\(Kanye_West_song\))" debuted at number four.[^cite_note-80] While it was criticized prior to release, *808s & Heartbreak* is considered to have had a significant effect on hip-hop music, encouraging other rappers to take more creative risks with their productions.[^cite_note-81]

While [Taylor Swift](5422144.md) was accepting her award for Best Female Video at the [2009 MTV Video Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanye_West_MTV_Video_Music_Awards_controversy), West went on stage and grabbed the microphone from her to proclaim that [Beyoncé](83688.md) deserved the award instead. He was subsequently removed from the remainder of the show for his actions.[^cite_note-Cnn.com-82][^cite_note-rollingstone.com-83][^cite_note-84] West was criticized by various celebrities for the outburst,[^cite_note-Cnn.com-82][^cite_note-85][^cite_note-latfallout-86][^cite_note-MTV-87] and by President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama), who called West a "jackass".[^cite_note-BBCquote-88][^cite_note-politico.com-89][^cite_note-people.com-90]\[^cite_note-[www.foxnews.com-91](http://www.foxnews.com-91/)] The incident sparked a large influx of [Internet photo memes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_meme).[^cite_note-92] West subsequently apologized,[^cite_note-latfallout-86][^cite_note-93] including personally to Swift.[^cite_note-MTV.com-94][^cite_note-MSNBC-95] However, in a November 2010 interview, he seemed to recant his past apologies, describing the act at the 2009 awards show as "selfless".[^cite_note-96][^cite_note-97]

### 2010–2012: *My Beautiful Dark Twisted Fantasy*, *Watch the Throne*, and *Cruel Summer*

Main articles: [*My Beautiful Dark Twisted Fantasy*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MBDTF), [*Watch the Throne*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watch_the_Throne), and [*Cruel Summer* (GOOD Music album)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cruel_Summer_\(GOOD_Music_album\))



West at the [SWU Music & Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SWU_Music_&_Arts) Festival in Brazil in 2011

Following the highly publicized incident, West took a brief break from music and threw himself into fashion, then returned to Hawaii for a few months writing and recording his next album.[^cite_note-Bever-98] Importing his favorite producers and artists to work on and inspire his recording, West kept engineers behind the boards 24 hours a day and slept only in increments. Noah Callahan-Bever, a writer for *[Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complex_\(magazine\))*, was present during the sessions and described the "communal" atmosphere as thus: "With the right songs and the right album, he can overcome any and all controversy, and we are here to contribute, challenge, and inspire."[^cite_note-Bever-98] A variety of artists contributed to the project, including close friends Jay-Z, Kid Cudi and [Pusha T](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pusha_T), as well as collaborations with artists including [Justin Vernon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Vernon) of [Bon Iver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bon_Iver) and [Gil Scott Heron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gil_Scott_Heron).[^cite_note-99]

*[My Beautiful Dark Twisted Fantasy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/My_Beautiful_Dark_Twisted_Fantasy)*, West's fifth studio album, was released in November 2010 to widespread acclaim from critics, many of whom considered it his best work and said it solidified his comeback.[^cite_note-100] In stark contrast to his previous effort, which featured a minimalist sound, *Dark Fantasy* adopts a maximalist philosophy and deals with themes of celebrity and excess.[^cite_note-Sheffield-59] The record included the international hit "[All of the Lights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_of_the_Lights)", and *Billboard* hits "[Power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_\(Kanye_West_song\))", "[Monster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monster_\(Kanye_West_song\))", and "[Runaway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Runaway_\(Kanye_West_song\))",[^cite_note-Hot100-101] the latter of which accompanied a [35-minute titular film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Runaway_\(2010_film\)) directed by and starring West.[^cite_note-BillboardPremiere-102] During this time, West initiated the free music program [GOOD Fridays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GOOD_Fridays) through his website, offering a free download of previously unreleased songs each Friday, a portion of which were included on the album. This promotion ran from August to December 2010.[^cite_note-103] *Dark Fantasy* went on to go platinum in the United States,[^cite_note-RIAA-104] but its omission as a contender for [Album of the Year](196513.md) at the [54th Grammy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/54th_Grammy_Awards) was viewed as a "snub" by several media outlets.[^cite_note-105]

In 2011, West went on a festival tour to commemorate the release of *My Beautiful Dark Twisted Fantasy*, performing and headlining at numerous festivals, including: [SWU Music & Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SWU_Music_&_Arts), [Austin City Limits Music Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austin_City_Limits_Music_Festival), [Oya Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%98yafestivalen), [Flow Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flow_Festival#2011), [Live Music Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Live_Music_Festival#2011), [The Big Chill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Big_Chill_\(music_festival\)#Past_line-ups), [Essence Music Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Essence_Music_Festival#2011), [Lollapalooza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Lollapalooza_lineups_by_year#2011), and [Coachella](494630.md), which was described by *[The Hollywood Reporter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hollywood_Reporter)* as "one of greatest hip-hop sets of all time".[^cite_note-106] West released the collaborative album *[Watch the Throne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watch_the_Throne)* with Jay-Z in August 2011. By employing a sales strategy that released the album digitally weeks before its physical counterpart, *Watch the Throne* became one of the few major label albums in the [Internet age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_age) to avoid a [leak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_leak).[^cite_note-107][^cite_note-108] "[Niggas in Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niggas_in_Paris)" became the record's highest-charting single, peaking at number five on the *Billboard* Hot 100.[^cite_note-Hot100-101] The co-headlining [Watch the Throne Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watch_the_Throne_Tour) kicked off in October 2011 and concluded in June 2012.[^cite_note-109] In 2012, West released the [compilation album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compilation_album) *[Cruel Summer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cruel_Summer_\(GOOD_Music_album\))*, a collection of tracks by artists from West's record label GOOD Music.[^cite_note-110]

### 2013–2015: *Yeezus* and the Yeezus Tour

Main articles: [*Yeezus*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeezus) and [The Yeezus Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Yeezus_Tour)

Sessions for West's sixth solo effort begin to take shape in early 2013 in his own personal loft's living room at a Paris hotel.[^cite_note-NewYorkTimes-111] Determined to "undermine the commercial",[^cite_note-sessions-112] he once again brought together close collaborators and attempted to incorporate Chicago [drill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drill_\(music_genre\)), [dancehall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dancehall), [acid house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acid_house), and [industrial music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_music).[^cite_note-chicagotribune-113] Primarily inspired by architecture,[^cite_note-NewYorkTimes-111] West's perfectionist tendencies led him to contact producer [Rick Rubin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rick_Rubin) fifteen days shy of its due date to strip down the record's sound in favor of a more minimalist approach.[^cite_note-Rstonerubin-114] Initial promotion of his sixth album included worldwide video projections of the album's music and live television performances.[^cite_note-115][^cite_note-snl-116] *[Yeezus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeezus)*, West's sixth album, was released June 18, 2013, to rave reviews from critics.[^cite_note-117] It became his sixth consecutive number one debut, but also marked his lowest solo opening week sales.[^cite_note-billboard123-118]



West on the [Yeezus Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeezus_Tour) in 2013

In September 2013, West announced he would be headlining [his first solo tour in five years](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Yeezus_Tour) to support *Yeezus*, with fellow American rapper [Kendrick Lamar](29909823.md) accompanying him as a supporting act.[^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120] The tour was met with rave reviews from critics.[^cite_note-rsyeezus-121] *[Rolling Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone)* described it as "crazily entertaining, hugely ambitious, emotionally affecting (really!) and, most importantly, totally bonkers".[^cite_note-rsyeezus-121] Zack O'Malley Greenburg of *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* praised West for "taking risks that few pop stars, if any, are willing to take in today's hyper-exposed world of pop", describing the show as "overwrought and uncomfortable at times, but \[it] excels at challenging norms and provoking thought in a way that just isn't common for mainstream musical acts of late".[^cite_note-Greenburg-122] West subsequently released a number of singles featuring [Paul McCartney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_McCartney), including "[Only One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Only_One_\(Kanye_West_song\))"[^cite_note-123] and "[FourFiveSeconds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FourFiveSeconds)", also featuring [Rihanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rihanna).[^cite_note-124]

In November 2013, West stated that he was beginning work on his next studio album, hoping to release it by mid-2014,[^cite_note-125] with production by Rick Rubin and [Q-Tip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Q-Tip_\(musician\)).[^cite_note-126] Having initially announced a new album entitled *[Yeezus II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeezus_II)* slated for a 2014 release, West announced in March 2015 that the album would instead be tentatively called *So Help Me God*.[^cite_note-127] In May 2015, West was awarded an [honorary doctorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honorary_doctorate) by the [School of the Art Institute of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_of_the_Art_Institute_of_Chicago) for his contributions to music, fashion, and popular culture.[^cite_note-128][^cite_note-129][^cite_note-131] The next month, West headlined at the [Glastonbury Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glastonbury_Festival) in the UK, despite a petition signed by almost 135,000 people against his appearance.[^cite_note-132] Another petition aimed to block West from headlining the [2015 Pan American Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Pan_American_Games), garnering 50,000 supporters.[^cite_note-133]

### 2016–2017: *The Life of Pablo* and tour cancellation

Main article: [*The Life of Pablo*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Life_of_Pablo)



West on the [Saint Pablo Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Pablo_Tour) in 2016

West announced in January 2016 that *Swish* would be released on February 11, and later that month, released new songs "[Real Friends](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Friends_\(Kanye_West_song\))" and a snippet of "[No More Parties in LA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No_More_Parties_in_LA)" with Kendrick Lamar. On January 26, 2016, West revealed he had renamed the album from *Swish* to *Waves*.[^cite_note-134] In the weeks leading up to the album's release, West became embroiled in several [Twitter](9988187.md) controversies.[^cite_note-dailybeast-135] Several days ahead of its release, West again changed the title, this time to *[The Life of Pablo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Life_of_Pablo)*.[^cite_note-136][^cite_note-137] On February 11, West premiered the album at [Madison Square Garden](75129.md) as part of the presentation of his Yeezy Season 3 clothing line.[^cite_note-pitchfork.com-138] Following the preview, West announced that he would be modifying the tracklist once more before its release to the public.[^cite_note-139] He released the album exclusively on [Tidal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tidal_\(service\)) on February 14, 2016, following a performance on *[Saturday Night Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saturday_Night_Live)*.[^cite_note-140][^cite_note-Pitchfork-141] Following its release, West continued to tinker with mixes of several tracks, describing the work as "a living breathing changing creative expression"[^cite_note-fader-142] and proclaiming the end of the [album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Album) as a dominant release form.[^cite_note-slate-143] Despite West's earlier comments, in addition to Tidal, the album was released through several other competing services starting in April.[^cite_note-144]

In February 2016, West stated on Twitter that he was planning to release another album in the summer of 2016, tentatively called *Turbo Grafx 16* in reference to the 1990s [video game console of the same name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TurboGrafx-16).[^cite_note-145][^cite_note-146] In June 2016, West released the collaborative lead single "[Champions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champions_\(GOOD_Music_song\))" off the GOOD Music album *Cruel Winter*, which has yet to be released.[^cite_note-6_Minute_Track-147][^cite_note-148] Later that month, West released a controversial video for "[Famous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Famous_\(Kanye_West_song\))", which depicted wax figures of several celebrities (including West, Kardashian, Taylor Swift, businessman and then-presidential candidate [Donald Trump](4848272.md), comedian [Bill Cosby](158894.md), and former president George W. Bush) sleeping nude in a shared bed.[^cite_note-149]

In August 2016, West embarked on the [Saint Pablo Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Pablo_Tour) in support of *The Life of Pablo.*[^cite_note-hhd-150] The performances featured a mobile stage suspended from the ceiling.[^cite_note-hhd-150] West postponed several dates in October following the [Paris robbery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Kardashian#Paris_robbery) of several of his wife's effects.[^cite_note-bbm-151] On November 21, 2016, West cancelled the remaining 21 dates on the Saint Pablo Tour, following a week of no-shows, curtailed concerts and rants about politics.[^cite_note-152] He was later admitted for psychiatric observation at [UCLA Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UCLA_Medical_Center).[^cite_note-153][^cite_note-154] He stayed hospitalized over the Thanksgiving weekend because of a temporary psychosis stemming from sleep deprivation and extreme dehydration.[^cite_note-155] Following this episode West took an 11-month break from posting on Twitter and the public in general.[^cite_note-156]

### 2017–2019: *Ye* and the Wyoming Sessions

Main article: [*Ye* (album)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ye_\(album\)) See also: [*Daytona* (album)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daytona_\(album\)), [*Kids See Ghosts* (album)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kids_See_Ghosts_\(album\)), [*Nasir* (album)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasir_\(album\)), and [*K.T.S.E.*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K.T.S.E.)



West and Kid Cudi (left) at a listening party for *[Kids See Ghosts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kids_See_Ghosts_\(album\))* in 2018

It was reported in May 2017 that West was recording new music in [Jackson Hole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jackson_Hole), Wyoming, with a wide range of collaborators.[^cite_note-157][^cite_note-158] In April 2018, West announced plans to write a philosophy book entitled *Break the Simulation*,[^cite_note-159] later clarifying that he was sharing the book "in real time" on Twitter and began posting content that was likened to "[life coaching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_coaching)".[^cite_note-Kanyelifecoach-160] Later that month, he also announced two new albums, a solo album and self-titled collaboration with Kid Cudi under the name [Kids See Ghosts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kids_See_Ghosts), both of which would be released in June.[^cite_note-161] Additionally, he revealed he would produce upcoming albums by GOOD Music label-mates [Pusha T](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pusha_T) and [Teyana Taylor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teyana_Taylor), as well as [Nas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nas).[^cite_note-162] Shortly thereafter, West released the non-album singles "[Lift Yourself](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lift_Yourself)" and "[Ye vs. the People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ye_vs._the_People)", in which he and [T.I.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T.I.) discusses West's support of Donald Trump.[^cite_note-163]

Pusha T's *[Daytona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daytona_\(album\))*, the first album to release from the Wyoming Sessions, was released in May to critical acclaim. Although, the album's artwork—a photograph of deceased singer [Whitney Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitney_Houston)'s bathroom that West paid \$85,000 to license—attracted controversy.[^cite_note-Recording-164][^cite_note-165] The following week, West released his eighth studio album, *[Ye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ye_\(album\))*. West has suggested that he scrapped the original recordings of the album and re-recorded it within a month.[^cite_note-166] The week after, West released a collaborative album with Kid Cudi as [Kids See Ghosts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kids_See_Ghosts), titled *[Kids See Ghosts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kids_See_Ghosts_\(album\))*. West also completed production work on Nas' *[Nasir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasir_\(album\))*[^cite_note-167] and Teyana Taylor's *[K.T.S.E.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K.T.S.E.)*, which were released in the same month.[^cite_note-168]

In September, West announced his ninth studio album *[Yandhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yandhi)* to be released by the end of the month, and another collaborative album with fellow Chicagoan rapper [Chance the Rapper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chance_the_Rapper), titled *Good Ass Job*. That same month, West announced that he would be changing his stage name to "Ye".[^cite_note-The_Guardian_29_September_2018-169] *Yandhi* was originally set for release in September 2018 but was postponed multiple times.[^cite_note-170] In January 2019, West pulled out of headlining that year's [Coachella festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coachella_festival) after negotiations broke down due to discord regarding stage design.[^cite_note-171] In July, it was reported that songs from West's unreleased album *Yandhi* were leaked online.[^cite_note-172] The following month, West's then-wife [Kim Kardashian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Kardashian) announced that his next album would be titled *[Jesus Is King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesus_Is_King)*, effectively scrapping *Yandhi*.[^cite_note-173][^cite_note-174] By October, the entire unfinished album was available for a short time on streaming services Spotify and Tidal.[^cite_note-175]

### 2019–2022: *Jesus Is King*, *Donda*, and *Donda 2*

Main articles: [*Jesus Is King*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesus_Is_King), [*Donda*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donda), and [*Donda 2*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donda_2)



West at the [Met Gala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Met_Gala) in 2019

On January 6, 2019, West started his weekly "[Sunday Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunday_Service_\(group\))" events, which included soul variations of both West's and others' songs and were attended by multiple celebrities, including the Kardashians, [Charlie Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlie_Wilson_\(singer\)), and Kid Cudi.[^cite_note-Hearst_Magazine_Media,_Inc.-176] West previewed a new song, "[Water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_\(Kanye_West_song\))" at his "Sunday Service" orchestration performance at Coachella 2019,[^cite_note-177] which was later revealed to feature on his upcoming album *[Jesus Is King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesus_Is_King)*;[^cite_note-track-178] West released the album on October 25, 2019.[^cite_note-Ryan2019-179] It became the first to ever top the *Billboard* 200, Top R\&B/Hip-Hop Albums, Top Rap Albums, Top Christian Albums and Top Gospel Albums at the same time.[^cite_note-180] On December 25, 2019, West and Sunday Service released *[Jesus Is Born](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesus_Is_Born)*, containing 19 songs, including several re-workings of older West songs.[^cite_note-181]

West released a single titled "[Wash Us in the Blood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wash_Us_in_the_Blood)" on June 30, 2020, featuring fellow American rapper and singer [Travis Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travis_Scott), along with the music video, which was set to serve as the lead single from his tenth studio album *[Donda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donda)*.[^cite_note-182] However, in September 2020, West stated that he would not be releasing any further music until he is "done with \[his] contract with Sony and Universal".[^cite_note-183] On October 16, he released the single "[Nah Nah Nah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nah_Nah_Nah)".[^cite_note-184] West held several listening parties at [Mercedes-Benz Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-Benz_Stadium) for his upcoming album *Donda* in the summer of 2021, where he had taken up temporary residence in one of the stadium's locker rooms, converting it into a recording studio to finish the recording.[^cite_note-185][^cite_note-186] After multiple delays, *Donda* was released on August 29, 2021.[^cite_note-187] West claimed the album was released early without his approval and alleged that Universal had altered the tracklist.[^cite_note-188] He released a deluxe edition of *Donda*, including five new songs, to streaming services on November 14, 2021.[^cite_note-189] On November 20, days after ending their long-running feud,[^cite_note-190] West and rapper [Drake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drake_\(musician\)) confirmed that they would stage the "Free [Larry Hoover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larry_Hoover)" benefit concert on December 9 at the [Los Angeles Memorial Coliseum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Memorial_Coliseum).[^cite_note-191]

On January 5, 2022, West was announced as one of the 2022 headliners of [Coachella Valley Music and Arts Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coachella_Valley_Music_and_Arts_Festival).[^cite_note-192] Later that month on January 15, West released the first single for his upcoming album *[Donda 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donda_2)*, "[Eazy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eazy_\(song\))" featuring [The Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Game_\(rapper\)),[^cite_note-193] to be executive produced by American rapper [Future](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Future_\(rapper\)).[^cite_note-ReleaseDate-194] West hosted a listening event for the album at [LoanDepot Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LoanDepot_Park) in Miami, Florida, on February 22.[^cite_note-195] In April, shortly before Coachella, West pulled out as headlining act,[^cite_note-196] then proceeded to pull out of headlining [Rolling Loud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Loud).[^cite_note-197] West and The Game performed the single on July 22, marking West's first performance in five months following the low profile he had been keeping since *Donda 2* remained unfinished.[^cite_note-198] A day later, despite cancelling as headliner, he appeared at Rolling Loud during [Lil Durk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lil_Durk)'s set.[^cite_note-199] In December 2022, after [weeks of controversial antisemitic statements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Views_of_Kanye_West#Antisemitism), West released a new song, "Someday We'll All Be Free", on his Instagram.[^cite_note-200]

### 2023–2024: *Vultures 1 and 2*

Main articles: [*Vultures 1*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vultures_1) and [*Vultures 2*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vultures_2)



¥\$ listening party in [Bologna, Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bologna,_Italy), for *[Vultures 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vultures_1)* (2024)

On August 25, 2023, West was reported to be in the process of recording his eleventh studio album, with two sources close to him stating that the release of new music was "imminent".[^cite_note-201] On October 13, *Billboard* reported that West had finished recording a [collaborative studio album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vultures_\(Kanye_West_and_Ty_Dolla_Sign_album\)) with [Ty Dolla Sign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ty_Dolla_Sign) and was in the process of shipping the album to distributors, adding that the album was originally intended for an official release that day but was ultimately pushed back for unknown reasons and expected to drop within the coming weeks.[^cite_note-202] The eponymous lead single for the album, "[Vultures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vultures_\(song\))" featuring [Bump J](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bump_J), was released on November 22, 2023.[^cite_note-203]

Throughout late 2023 and early 2024, West and Ty Dolla Sign held several concerts previewing songs from the album. In a trailer, West announced that *Vultures* would be released as a trilogy of albums, with three volumes set to be released on February 9, March 6, and April 5, 2024. In January 2024, West co-signed [4Batz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4Batz) and called him his favorite new artist.[^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205] On February 8, 2024, West released the first volume's second single, "[Talking / Once Again](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talking_/_Once_Again)" featuring his eldest daughter North.[^cite_note-206][^cite_note-207] A day later, producer [Erick Sermon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erick_Sermon) revealed in an interview that West's upcoming eleventh studio album was titled *Y3*, while also stating that he had contributed to the album in 2023.[^cite_note-208] West has since denied working on an album titled *Y3*.[^cite_note-209]

On February 10, 2024, hours after West held a listening party at UBS Arena, the first volume of the *Vultures* trilogy, entitled *[Vultures 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vultures_1)*, was officially released.[^cite_note-210][^cite_note-211] The album debuted at number one on the *Billboard* 200, becoming West's eleventh consecutive and Ty Dolla Sign's first number one album, respectively.[^cite_note-\:02-212] *[Vultures 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vultures_2)* released later that year on August 3.[^cite_note-213]

### 2025–present: *Cuck* and *Bully*

Main articles: [*Cuck* (album)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuck_\(album\)), [*Bully* (album)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bully_\(album\)), and [Ye Live Concert Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ye_Live_Concert_Tour)

On September 28, 2024, during a concert in [Haikou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haikou), China at the [Wuyuan River Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wuyuan_River_Stadium), West announced his eleventh solo album *[Bully](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bully_\(album\))*, debuting the track "[Beauty and the Beast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beauty_and_the_Beast_\(Kanye_West_song\))".[^cite_note-214][^cite_note-215] On March 15, 2025, he shared a song on his [X](9988187.md) account, "[Lonely Roads Still Go to Sunshine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lonely_Roads_Still_Go_to_Sunshine)", featuring his daughter North and vocals credited to [Sean Combs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sean_Combs), resulting in legal action from Kim Kardashian.[^cite_note-216] On March 18, he shared links on X to three versions of *Bully*, all of which were accompanied by a short film starring his son Saint.[^cite_note-217] He stated that the album was "not finished and half of the vocals \[are] [AI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AI)", and added that he did not intend to release it on streaming services because of "French and Jewish record labels".[^cite_note-218][^cite_note-219] *Bully* would later be released as the "screening version" on [YouTube](3524766.md) for a temporary time.[^cite_note-220]

On March 26, 2025, West released "[WW3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WW3_\(Kanye_West_song\))" under the name Ye onto streaming services, after having the song been previously teased by [Dave Blunts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dave_Blunts) and [Adin Ross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adin_Ross).[^cite_note-raptvww3-221] A week later, [DJ Akademiks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DJ_Akademiks) announced that West was working on *WW3*, a collaborative album with Blunts,[^cite_note-HNHH:_WestBlunts-222] a few days after they held an interview where West wore a black [Ku Klux Klan](16779.md)-inspired outfit and said further controversial remarks.[^cite_note-vibeww3-223][^cite_note-billboardakademiks-224] West changed the album's title to *Cuck* on April 21,[^cite_note-225] and in the following weeks released the singles "[Cousins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cousins_\(Kanye_West_song\))" and "[Heil Hitler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heil_Hitler_\(song\))". After *Cuck* leaked on May 18, 2025,[^cite_note-226] West stated that he was "done with antisemitism", and asked God to "forgive me for the pain I've caused". West removed pro-[Nazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi) and antisemitic lyrics from the *Cuck* singles, and on June 22, announced he was changing the album's title to *In a Perfect World*; the album was ultimately scrapped.[^cite_note-227]



West performed at the [SoFi Stadium](45525802.md) in April 2026, following the release of *[Bully](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bully_\(album\))*, selling out with \$33 million and being his first concert in Los Angeles since 2021.

On April 29, West released *Donda 2* on streaming services.[^cite_note-228] On May 21, he released the single "[Alive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alive_\(Kanye_West_and_YoungBoy_Never_Broke_Again_song\))", featuring [YoungBoy Never Broke Again](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YoungBoy_Never_Broke_Again), as the theme song for his upcoming YZY SZN 10 fashion collection.[^cite_note-YeNBAAlive-229] On June 20, West released a three-track [extended play](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extended_play) (EP) preview of *Bully* on streaming services.[^cite_note-230] On June 27, West released *[Never Stop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Never_Stop_\(EP\))*, an EP by Sean Combs' son Christian "King" Combs. West served as *Never Stop*'s executive producer, and North appears on the song "[Lonely Roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lonely_Roads)". The EP's release coincided with the closing arguments of [Sean Combs' criminal trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trial_of_Sean_Combs), in which he was charged with [racketeering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racketeering) and [sex trafficking](2101268.md); West has been an outspoken defender of Combs.[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232]

On January 3, 2026, after multiple delays, [Yeezy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeezy_\(brand\)) updated its website with pre-orders to *Bully* including multi-colored vinyl, cassette, CD, bundles, and digital download.[^cite_note-\:32-233] West's team announced that the album would no longer feature [AI-generated vocals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_and_artificial_intelligence).[^cite_note-234] *Bully* was released digitally on March 28, following West's deal to sign with American media company [Gamma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gamma_\(media_company\)).[^cite_note-Paul-2026-235][^cite_note-236] Following performances at [Shanghai](27643.md) in July 2025[^cite_note-237] and Mexico in January 2026,[^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239] On April 1 and 3, West performed two concerts at [SoFi Stadium](45525802.md) in Los Angeles,[^cite_note-240][^cite_note-241] selling \$33 million in ticket sales[^cite_note-242] and marking his first performance in the city since 2021.[^cite_note-243]

## Musical style



West performing in 2012

West's musical career is defined by frequent stylistic shifts and different musical approaches.[^cite_note-amg-5] In the subsequent years since his debut, West has both musically and lyrically taken an increasingly [experimental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Experimental_music) approach to crafting [progressive hip-hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive_hip-hop) music while maintaining accessible [pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_music) sensibilities.[^cite_note-Gates,_Henry_Louis,_et_al._2010_705–713-244][^cite_note-Caramanica-245][^cite_note-Rob_Tannenbaum_2006_p._49-246] Ed Ledsham of *[PopMatters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PopMatters)* said that "West's melding of multiple genres into the hip-hop fold is a complex act that challenges the dominant white notions of what constitutes true ['art' music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_music)."[^cite_note-247] West's rhymes have been described as funny, provocative and articulate, capable of seamlessly segueing from shrewd commentary to comical [braggadocio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Braggadocio_\(rap\)) to introspective sensitivity.[^cite_note-McCormick-248] West imparts that he strives to speak in an inclusive manner so groups from different racial and gender backgrounds can comprehend his lyrics, saying he desired to sound "just as ill as [Jadakiss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jadakiss) and just as understandable as [Will Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Will_Smith)".[^cite_note-Vh1-249] Early in his career, West pioneered a style of hip-hop production dubbed "chipmunk-soul",[^cite_note-250][^cite_note-Cowie,_Del-251] a sampling technique involving the manipulation of tempo in order to [chop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chopping_\(sampling_technique\)) and stretch [pitched-up](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pitch_\(music\)) samples from vintage soul songs.[^cite_note-king-252][^cite_note-Ryan-253]

On his debut studio album, *[The College Dropout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_College_Dropout)* (2004), West formed the constitutive elements of his style, described as intricate hip-hop beats, topical subject matter, and clumsy rapping laced with inventive wordplay.[^cite_note-Gates,_Henry_Louis,_et_al._2010_705–713-244][^cite_note-auto1-254] The record saw West diverge from the then-dominant [gangster persona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gangsta_rap) in hip-hop in favor of more diverse, topical lyrical subjects,[^cite_note-ebony-255] including higher education, materialism, [self-consciousness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-consciousness), [minimum-wage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimum-wage) labor, [institutional prejudice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institutional_racism), [class struggle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Class_struggle), family, sexuality, his struggles in the music industry, and [middle-class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle-class) upbringing.[^cite_note-Love-256][^cite_note-257][^cite_note-Caramanica-245] Over time, West has explored a variety of [music genres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_genre), encompassing and taking inspiration from [chamber pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamber_pop) on his second studio album, *[Late Registration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Registration)* (2005),[^cite_note-Pilot-62] [arena rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arena_rock) and [europop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Europop) on his third album, *[Graduation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graduation_\(album\))* (2007),[^cite_note-Greene-258][^cite_note-Loop-259] [synth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synthpop)-driven [electropop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electropop) on his fourth album, *[808s & Heartbreak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/808s_&_Heartbreak)* (2008),[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261] [acid-house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acid-house), [drill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drill_\(music_genre\)), [industrial rap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_rap) and [trap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trap_music) on *[Yeezus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeezus)* (2013),[^cite_note-chicagotribune-113][^cite_note-atlantic-262] [gospel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gospel_\(music\)) and [Christian rap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_rap) on *[The Life of Pablo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Life_of_Pablo)* (2016), *[Jesus Is King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesus_Is_King)* (2019) and *[Donda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donda)* (2021),[^cite_note-263][^cite_note-rs-wip-264][^cite_note-rs-detroit-265][^cite_note-ConsequenceReview-266] and [psychedelic music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychedelic_music) on *[Kids See Ghosts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kids_See_Ghosts_\(album\))* (2018).[^cite_note-267]

## Other ventures

### Fashion

Early in his career, West made clear his interest in fashion and desire to work in the clothing design industry.[^cite_note-amg-5][^cite_note-NewYorkTimes-111] He launched his own clothing line in spring 2006,[^cite_note-268] and developed it over the following four years before the line was ultimately cancelled in 2009.[^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270] In January 2007, West's first sneaker collaboration was released, a special-edition Bapesta from [A Bathing Ape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Bathing_Ape).[^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272] In 2009, West collaborated with [Nike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike,_Inc.) to release his own shoe, the [Air Yeezys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike_Air_Yeezy), becoming the first non-athlete to be given a shoe deal with the company.[^cite_note-273] In January 2009, he introduced his first shoe line designed for [Louis Vuitton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Vuitton) during [Paris Fashion Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Fashion_Week). The line was released in summer 2009.[^cite_note-274] West has additionally designed shoewear for Italian shoemaker [Giuseppe Zanotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Zanotti).[^cite_note-275]

In fall 2009, West moved to [Rome](25458.md), where he interned at Italian fashion brand [Fendi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fendi), giving ideas for the men's collection.[^cite_note-276] In March 2011, West collaborated with [M/M Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M/M_Paris) for a series of silk scarves featuring artwork from *My Beautiful Dark Twisted Fantasy*.[^cite_note-277] In October 2011, West premiered his women's fashion label at Paris Fashion Week.[^cite_note-TIFDb-278] His debut fashion show received mixed-to-negative reviews.[^cite_note-279] In March 2012, West premiered a second fashion line at Paris Fashion Week.[^cite_note-280][^cite_note-281] Critics deemed the sophomore effort "much improved" compared to his first show.[^cite_note-282]

On December 3, 2013, [Adidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas) officially confirmed a new shoe collaboration deal with West.[^cite_note-Little-283] After months of anticipation and rumors, West confirmed the release of the [Adidas Yeezy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas_Yeezy) Boosts. In 2015, West unveiled a Yeezy clothing line, premiering in collaboration with Adidas early that year.[^cite_note-284] In June 2016, Adidas announced a new long-term contract with Kanye West that extended the Yeezy line to a number of stores, planning to sell sports performance products like basketball, football, and soccer,[^cite_note-newcontract-285] although Adidas terminated the partnership with West in October 2022 due to his antisemitic remarks.[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287] In May 2021, West signed a 10-year deal linking Yeezy with [Gap Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gap_Inc.) to create [Yeezy Gap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeezy_Gap), however, in September 2022, West announced that he was ending the deal.[^cite_note-288]

### Business ventures

West founded the record label and production company [GOOD Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GOOD_Music) in 2004, in conjunction with [Sony BMG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sony_BMG), shortly after releasing his debut album, *[The College Dropout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_College_Dropout)*. West, alongside then-unknown [Ohio](22199.md) singer [John Legend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Legend) and fellow Chicago rapper [Common](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_\(rapper\)) were the label's inaugural artists.[^cite_note-Founded-289] The label houses artists including West, [Big Sean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Sean), Pusha T, Teyana Taylor, Yasiin Bey / Mos Def, [D'banj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D'banj) and John Legend, and producers including [Hudson Mohawke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hudson_Mohawke), Q-Tip, [Travis Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travis_Scott), No I.D., [Jeff Bhasker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Bhasker), and S1. GOOD Music has released ten albums [certified gold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RIAA_certification) or higher by the [Recording Industry Association of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recording_Industry_Association_of_America) (RIAA). In November 2015, West appointed Pusha T the new president of GOOD Music.[^cite_note-290]

In August 2008, West revealed plans to open 10 [Fatburger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatburger) restaurants in the Chicago area after his company, KW Foods LLC, bought the rights to the chain in Chicago.[^cite_note-291] The first location opened in September 2008 in Orland Park and a second followed in January 2009.[^cite_note-292] The Orland Park location closed in early 2011 due to poor sales performance.[^cite_note-293]

In January 2012, West announced his establishment of the creative content company [Donda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donda_\(company\)), named after his late mother.[^cite_note-294] In his announcement, West proclaimed that the company would "pick up where [Steve Jobs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Jobs) left off"; Donda would operate as a "design company" with a goal to "make products and experiences that people want and can afford".[^cite_note-vh1.com-295] In stating Donda's creative philosophy, West articulated the need to "put creatives in a room together with like minds" in order to "simplify and aesthetically improve everything we see, taste, touch, and feel".[^cite_note-vh1.com-295] West is notoriously secretive about the company's operations, maintaining neither an official website nor a social media presence.[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297] Contemporary critics have noted the consistent minimalistic aesthetic exhibited throughout Donda creative projects.[^cite_note-298][^cite_note-299][^cite_note-300]

West expressed interest in starting an architecture firm in May 2013, saying "I want to do product, I am a product person, not just clothing but water bottle design, architecture ... I make music but I shouldn't be limited to one place of creativity"[^cite_note-301][^cite_note-302] and then later in November 2013, delivering a [manifesto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manifesto) on his architectural goals during a visit to [Harvard Graduate School of Design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_Graduate_School_of_Design).[^cite_note-303] In May 2018, West announced he was starting an architecture firm called Yeezy Home, which will act as an arm of his already successful Yeezy fashion label.[^cite_note-304] In June 2018, the first Yeezy Home collaboration was announced by designer Jalil Peraza, teasing an affordable concrete [prefabricated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prefabricated) home as part of a [social housing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_housing) project.[^cite_note-305][^cite_note-306]

In March 2015, it was announced that West is a co-owner, with various other music artists, in the [music streaming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_streaming) service [Tidal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tidal_\(service\)). The service specialises in [lossless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lossless) audio and high definition music videos. Jay-Z acquired the parent company of Tidal, [Aspiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aspiro), in the first quarter of 2015.[^cite_note-307] Sixteen artist stakeholders including Jay-Z, Rihanna, Beyoncé, Madonna, [Chris Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Martin), [Nicki Minaj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicki_Minaj) co-own Tidal, with the majority owning a 3% equity stake.[^cite_note-308] In October 2022, in response to bans he received on Twitter and Instagram[^cite_note-309] stemming from his antisemitic comments,[^cite_note-310] West reached an [agreement in principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agreement_in_principle) to acquire the [alt-tech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alt-tech) social network [Parler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parler) for an undisclosed amount.[^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312] Parler and West mutually agreed to terminate the proposed deal in mid-November.[^cite_note-313]

### Philanthropy

West, alongside his mother, founded the Kanye West Foundation in Chicago in 2003, tasked with a mission to battle dropout and illiteracy rates, while partnering with community organizations to provide underprivileged youth access to music education.[^cite_note-Mission_Statement-314] In 2007, West and the Foundation partnered with [Strong American Schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strong_American_Schools) as part of their "Ed in '08" campaign.[^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316] As spokesman for the campaign, West appeared in a series of PSAs for the organization, and hosted an inaugural benefit concert in August of that year.[^cite_note-317] In 2008, following the death of West's mother, the foundation was rechristened The Dr. Donda West Foundation.[^cite_note-Mission_Statement-314][^cite_note-318] The foundation ceased operations in 2011.[^cite_note-319] In 2013, Kanye West and friend [Rhymefest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhymefest) founded Donda's House, Inc., a program aimed at helping at-risk Chicago youth.[^cite_note-320]

West has contributed to hurricane relief in 2005 by participating in a [Hurricane Katrina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Katrina) benefit concert after the storm had ravaged black communities in [New Orleans](53842.md)[^cite_note-321] and in 2012 when he performed at a [Hurricane Sandy benefit concert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/12-12-12:_The_Concert_for_Sandy_Relief).[^cite_note-322] In January 2019, West donated \$10 million towards the completion of the [Roden Crater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roden_Crater) by American artist [James Turrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Turrell).[^cite_note-323] In June 2020, in the wake of the [murder of George Floyd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murder_of_George_Floyd) and the [following protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Floyd_protests), he donated \$2 million between the family of Floyd and other victims of [police brutality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_brutality), [Ahmaud Arbery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmaud_Arbery) and [Breonna Taylor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shooting_of_Breonna_Taylor). The donation funded legal fees for Arbery and Taylor's families, as well as establishing a [529 plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/529_plan) to fully cover [college tuition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_tuition) for Floyd's daughter.[^cite_note-324]

### Acting and filmmaking

West made [cameo appearances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameo_appearances) as himself in the films *[State Property 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Property_2)* (2005) and *[The Love Guru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Love_Guru)* (2008),[^cite_note-325][^cite_note-MTV_Comedy_Lover-326] and in an episode of the television show *[Entourage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entourage_\(American_TV_series\))* in 2007.[^cite_note-327] West provided the voice for "Kenny West", a rapper, in the animated sitcom *[The Cleveland Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Cleveland_Show)* (2010–2012).[^cite_note-MTV_Comedy_Lover-326][^cite_note-328] In 2009, he starred in the [Spike Jonze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spike_Jonze)-directed short film *[We Were Once a Fairytale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/We_Were_Once_a_Fairytale)* (2009), playing himself acting belligerently while drunk in a nightclub.[^cite_note-329] West wrote, directed, and starred in the musical short film *[Runaway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Runaway_\(2010_film\))* (2010), which heavily features music from *My Beautiful Dark Twisted Fantasy*.[^cite_note-BillboardPremiere-102] The film depicts a relationship between a man, played by West, and a half-woman, half-[phoenix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoenix_\(mythology\)) creature.[^cite_note-vulture-330]

In 2012, West co-directed another short film, along with [Alexandre Moors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandre_Moors),[^cite_note-331] titled *[Cruel Summer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cruel_Summer_\(2012_film\))*, which premiered at the [2012 Cannes Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Cannes_Film_Festival) in a custom pyramid-shaped screening pavilion featuring seven screens constructed for the film. The film was meant to tie in with the compilation album [of the same name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cruel_Summer_\(GOOD_Music_album\)) to be released later that year.[^cite_note-332] West made a cameo appearance in the comedy *[Anchorman 2: The Legend Continues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anchorman_2:_The_Legend_Continues)* (2013) as a [MTV News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_News) representative in the film's fight scene.[^cite_note-333] In September 2018, West announced the starting of a film production company named Half Beast, LLC.[^cite_note-334] A documentary shot over 21 years featuring footage of West's early days in Chicago through the death of his mother to his presidential run was announced to debut in 2021. Titled *[Jeen-Yuhs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeen-Yuhs)*, it was acquired by [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix) for \$30 million[^cite_note-335] and released in 2022.[^cite_note-336] A documentary shot over six years with a billion-dollar budget was announced in 2024.[^cite_note-337][^cite_note-338] Titled *[In Whose Name?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In_Whose_Name%3F)*, it was directed by Nico Ballesteros and was released on September 19, 2025.[^cite_note-339]

### Presidential campaigns

Main article: [Kanye West presidential campaigns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanye_West_presidential_campaigns)

#### 2020



West at his first campaign rally in [North Charleston, South Carolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Charleston,_South_Carolina), on July 19, 2020

On July 4, 2020, West announced on Twitter that he would be running in the [2020 presidential election](48410011.md).[^cite_note-340][^cite_note-341] On July 7, West was interviewed by *Forbes* about his presidential run, where he announced that his running mate would be Wyoming preacher Michelle Tidball, and that he would run as an [independent](2172378.md) under the "Birthday Party", explaining his decision of why he chose the name, saying, "Because when we win, it's everybody's 'birthday'."[^cite_note-342] West also said he no longer supported Trump because he "hid in \[a] bunker" during the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md).[^cite_note-France-343] Continuing, he said, "You know? Obama's special. Trump's special. We say Kanye West is special. America needs special people that lead. [Bill Clinton](3356.md)? Special. [Joe Biden](145422.md)'s not special."[^cite_note-France-343]

Various political pundits speculated that West's presidential run was a publicity stunt to promote his latest music releases.[^cite_note-344] On July 15, 2020, official paperwork was filed with the [Federal Election Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Election_Commission) for West, under the "BDY" Party affiliation[^cite_note-345] amid claims that he was preparing to drop out.[^cite_note-346] West held his first rally that weekend, on July 19.[^cite_note-347] West aligned himself with the philosophy of a [consistent life ethic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consistent_life_ethic), a tenet of [Christian democracy](328969.md).[^cite_note-PCR2020-348] His platform advocated for the creation of a [culture of life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_life), endorsing [environmental stewardship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stewardship_\(theology\)), supporting the arts, buttressing [faith-based organizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faith-based_organization), restoring [school prayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_prayer), providing for a strong national defense, and ["America First" diplomacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/America_First_\(policy\)).[^cite_note-Kornhaber2020-349] In July 2020, West told *Forbes* that he is ignorant on issues such as taxes and foreign policy.[^cite_note-350]

West conceded on Twitter on November 4, 2020.[^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352] He received 66,365 votes in the 12 states he had ballot access in, receiving an average of 0.32%. Reported write-in votes gave West an additional 3,931 votes across 5 states. In addition, the *[Roque De La Fuente](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roque_De_La_Fuente) / Kanye West* ticket won 60,160 votes [in California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_presidential_election_in_California) (0.34%).[^cite_note-353] According to *[Reuters](18998750.md)*, on January 4, 2021, a Kanye West-linked publicist pressured a Georgia election worker to confess to bogus charges of election tampering to assist [Trump's claims of election interference](65905202.md).[^cite_note-354][^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356] In December 2021, *[The Daily Beast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Daily_Beast)* reported that West's presidential campaign received millions of dollars in services from a secret network of Republican operatives, payments to which the committee did not report. According to campaign finance experts, this was done to conceal a connection.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358]

#### 2024

West stated his intention to run for president again in the [2024 presidential election](59568546.md), saying at a November 2019 event, "When I run for president in 2024, we would've created so many jobs that I'm not going to run, I'm going to walk." He was met with laughter from the audience.[^cite_note-359] In response to a request for additional information from the [Federal Election Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Election_Commission) regarding the creation of a [presidential exploratory committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_exploratory_committee), West's representatives emphasized that he "has not decided whether to become a candidate for president in the 2024 election" and refused to file additional paperwork with the commission.[^cite_note-360] West officially launched his presidential campaign in November 2022.[^cite_note-361] West claimed to have asked former president Donald Trump to be his running mate; according to West, Trump was "caught \[...] off-guard" by the request and warned him of losing if he decided to run.[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363] In October 2023, an attorney for West said that he "is not a candidate for office in 2024".[^cite_note-364]

## Public image

West has been an outspoken and controversial celebrity throughout his career, receiving criticism from the mainstream media, industry colleagues and entertainers, and three U.S. presidents.[^cite_note-amg-5][^cite_note-NewYorkTimes-111] His conduct has been characterized as [trolling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trolling) or [rage-baiting](71669030.md).[^cite_note-DashTroll-365][^cite_note-ForbesTrolling-366][^cite_note-367] In 2024, Damon Dash said: "\[West]'s a person that pulls triggers... It's one [publicity stunt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Publicity_stunt) after another."[^cite_note-DashTroll-365] In the 2025 documentary *[In Whose Name?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In_Whose_Name%3F)*, West said that [his bipolar disorder](#Mental_health) allows him to portray his controversial statements and actions as [performance art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Performance_art).[^cite_note-368]

West's public persona, which media outlets have described as arrogant, egocentric, and [narcissistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narcissistic), has garnered both praise and criticism;[^cite_note-369][^cite_note-MicLoveKanye-370] *[The Dallas Morning News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Dallas_Morning_News)* described him as "the most polarizing artist in the world".[^cite_note-371] *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* said: "Everything that happens to Kanye adds to his outsized legend. And everywhere he goes, it seems, the man ... finds people glued to their computers in protest".[^cite_note-GuardianUnpredictable-372] His performances have sparked protests; an unsuccessful petition to cancel his performance at the [2015 Pan American Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Pan_American_Games)' closing ceremony received 54,000 signatures.[^cite_note-GuardianUnpredictable-372] West has been the subject of [internet memes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_meme) about how much he loves himself, which he referenced in the 2016 song "[I Love Kanye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Love_Kanye)".[^cite_note-MicLoveKanye-370] *[Spin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spin_\(magazine\))* wrote that "there is no shortage of Kanye West... making wildly passionate claims about his creative genius", such as a 2013 interview in which he ranted that his artistry was comparable to [William Shakespeare](32897.md)'s.[^cite_note-373]
